\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\tfrac{1}{c}+2\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime}),

whenever the right-hand side is sufficiently small.
We now define
\alpha_{\infty}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
with marginals
\xi_{i},\xi_{i,j},i\in\{1,2\},j\in\mathbb{N}
.
For this, we extend each sum coupling in
\Gamma^{1}(\xi_{i},\xi_{i,0},\xi_{i,1},\ldots)
by the coordinate
\bar{\upsilon}_{i}\coloneqq\upsilon_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\upsilon_{i,j}
and glue it to
\alpha
along the common marginal given by the
i
-th component of
\bar{\xi}
, i.e., the joint law of
(\bar{x}_{i},\bar{\upsilon}_{i})
; the countably many coordinates are handled via the Kolmogorov extension theorem as in
Remark

A.4
.
This coupling satisfies

\displaystyle\heartsuit

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Biggl(\left\langle\upsilon_{1}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\upsilon_{1,j},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left\langle\upsilon_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\upsilon_{2,j},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\Biggr)\mathrm{d}\alpha_{\infty}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1,0},\ldots,{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{2,0},\ldots,{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1}(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{1,j},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1,j}(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1,j},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2,j},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2,j}(\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{2,j},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}),

with
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i,j},i\in\{1,2\}
marginalizations of
\alpha_{\infty}
so that
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha_{i}}=\xi_{i},{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\alpha_{i}}=\mu_{i}\in\Omega_{i},{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha_{i,j}}=\xi_{i,j},{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\alpha_{i,j}}=\mu_{i}\in\Omega_{i}
.
We start by upper bounding the terms in the infinite sum.
By
[
35
, Theorem 10.2.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\mu,\hat{\mu}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\hat{\mu}\right)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle x_{2}-x_{1},x_{3}-x_{1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\theta(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x_{1}-x_{3}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

for all
\theta\in\Gamma(\gamma,\mu)
and for every
\gamma\in\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu},\hat{\mu})
. With
\hat{\mu}=\tilde{\mu}_{i,j}
,
\bar{\mu}=\bar{\mu}_{i}
, and
\mu=\mu_{i}
, we conclude

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle\left\langle\upsilon_{i,j},\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{i,j}(\bar{x}_{i},\upsilon_{i,j},\upsilon_{i}^{\prime})

(D.1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{i},\tilde{\mu}_{i,j}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\mu_{i},\tilde{\mu}_{i,j}\right)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{i,j}(\bar{x}_{i},\upsilon_{i,j},\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}).

For the other two terms, we now prove

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle\left\langle\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1}(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime})+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

(D.2)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\mu}_{1},\mu_{2}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1}(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{c}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}).

By definition of
\xi_{i}
, on the support of
\xi_{i}
, and so also on that of
\alpha_{i}
, it holds
\upsilon_{1}=\tfrac{1}{c}(\bar{x}_{2}-\bar{x}_{1}-a)
and
\upsilon_{2}=\tfrac{1}{c}(\bar{x}_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}+a)
for
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})
in the support of
\gamma\in\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
.
In particular
\upsilon_{2}=-\upsilon_{1}
and
\bar{x}_{1}+a-\bar{x}_{2}=-c\upsilon_{1}=c\upsilon_{2}
, and the transport plan
{(\pi_{1}+a,\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\gamma}
is optimal between
{(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}}
and
\bar{\mu}_{2}
.
On
\supp\xi_{1}
we have
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{1}+a+c\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\xi_{1}}=\gamma
, so augmenting
\alpha_{1}
with the deterministic coordinate
\bar{x}_{2}\coloneqq\bar{x}_{1}+a+c\upsilon_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
with
\bar{x}_{1}\coloneqq\bar{x}_{2}-a+c\upsilon_{2}
produces two transport plans with common
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})
-marginal
\gamma
.
Gluing them along this joint marginal yields
\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, on which
\bar{x}_{1}+a-\bar{x}_{2}=-c\upsilon_{1}=c\upsilon_{2}
holds almost everywhere by construction, such that

{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3},\pi_{5})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}=\alpha_{1}
,

{(\pi_{2},\pi_{4},\pi_{6})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}=\alpha_{2}
, and

{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}=\gamma
.
The marginals of
\tilde{\alpha}
corresponding to the displacement variables satisfy
{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{5})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}=\mu_{1}
and
{(\pi_{2}+\pi_{6})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}=\mu_{2}
by construction of
\alpha_{i}
.
Then
{(\pi_{1}+a+\pi_{5},\,\pi_{2}+\pi_{6})}_{\#}{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\Gamma({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\mu_{1}},\mu_{2})
, which yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\mu_{1}},\mu_{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\bar{x}_{1}+a+\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}-\bar{x}_{2}-\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}-\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{x}_{1}+a-\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle-\left\langle\bar{x}_{1}+a-\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}-\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad-c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad-c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\rangle+\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\tilde{\alpha}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{1}^{\prime},\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}).

where, for the second equality, we realize that, on the support of
\alpha_{i}
and so also on that of
\tilde{\alpha}
,
\bar{x}_{1}+a-\bar{x}_{2}=-c\upsilon_{1}=c\upsilon_{2}
.
Rearranging the terms, marginalizing, and dividing by
c>0
yields (
D.2
).
Combining (
D.2
) and (
D.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\heartsuit

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{1}{2}W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)^{2}+W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}\right)^{2}-W_{2}\left(\mu_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)^{2}-W_{2}\left(\mu_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{1}{c}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})-W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)^{2}+W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}\right)^{2}-W_{2}\left(\mu_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)^{2}-W_{2}\left(\mu_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{1}{c}+2\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(c)}}{=}\psi(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})-\psi(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{1}{c}+2\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(d)}}{\leq}\left(\frac{1}{c}+2\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime}),

where (a) combines (
D.1
) and (
D.2
); (b) uses the elementary bound
\tfrac{1}{c}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}s^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}t^{2}\bigr)\leq s-t
, valid for
s=c=W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
and all
t=W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\geq 0
(the bound follows from
-\tfrac{(s-t)^{2}}{2c}\leq 0
), together with
\tfrac{1}{c}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}=\tfrac{1}{c}+2
; (c) is the definition of
\psi
; and (d) is the local optimality of
(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
for
\psi
, applicable because the transport plan
(\bar{x}_{i},\bar{x}_{i}+\upsilon_{i}^{\prime})
gives
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{i},\mu_{i}\right)^{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})
, so that a small right-hand side keeps
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
within the neighborhood where
\psi
is minimized at
(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
.
Maximizing
\heartsuit
over the couplings
\alpha
realizing a given variation
\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{5},\pi_{6})}_{\#}{\alpha}
of
\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
at
(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
, with
\left\|\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\right\|_{(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\upsilon_{2}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},{\upsilon}_{1}^{\prime},{\upsilon}_{2}^{\prime})
, gives
\left\langle\bar{\xi},\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\right\rangle_{(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})}\leq\bigl(\tfrac{1}{c}+2\bigr)\left\|\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\right\|_{(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})}^{2}=o\left(\left\|\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\right\|_{(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})}\right)
for
\left\|\bar{\xi}^{\prime}\right\|_{(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})}
small.
Since
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\in\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
and
\bar{\xi}^{\prime}
were arbitrary,
\bar{\xi}
belongs to the strong regular normal cone
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}}((\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2}))
.

Step 6 (
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
are
\varepsilon
-close to the strong regular normal cones).

We show that
\xi_{i}
is
\varepsilon
-close to the strong regular normal cone
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{i}}(\bar{\mu}_{i})
. Clearly, by Step 5 above,
\xi_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\xi_{i,j}
lies in
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{i}}(\bar{\mu}_{i})
.
Fix the sum coupling
\alpha_{1}^{\star}\in\Gamma^{1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{1,0},\xi_{1,1},\ldots)
used to build the first component of
\bar{\xi}
in Step 3. By definition,
\alpha_{1}^{\star}
is a measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\left(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
with coordinates
(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1,0},\upsilon_{1,1},\ldots)
and
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}^{\star}}=\xi_{1}
and
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{j+3})}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}^{\star}}=\xi_{1,j}
for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Writing
\bar{\upsilon}_{1}\coloneqq\upsilon_{1}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\upsilon_{1,j}
for the induced arrow (as in Step 5), the first component of
\bar{\xi}
can be expressed as
\bar{\xi}_{1}={(\pi_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{1})}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}^{\star}}
.
Consider the pushforward

\tilde{\gamma}\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1})}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}^{\star}}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Its marginals are
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\tilde{\gamma}}=\xi_{1}
and
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\tilde{\gamma}}=\bar{\xi}_{1}
, and so
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma^{1}(\xi_{1},\bar{\xi}_{1})
.
Thus,

\displaystyle W_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\left(\xi_{1},\bar{\xi}_{1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\left(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)}\left\|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\upsilon_{1,j}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1}^{\star}(\bar{x}_{1},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{1,0},\upsilon_{1,1},\ldots).

Taking the square root and using the triangle inequality in the space
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\times_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{R}^{d});\alpha_{1}^{\star})
upper bounds the above by

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\left\|\xi_{1,j}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}2^{-j/2}<\varepsilon
.
Analogously,
W_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\left(\xi_{2},\xi_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\xi_{2,j}\right)<\varepsilon
, proving that
\xi_{i}
is
\varepsilon
-close to the strong regular normal cone
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{i}}(\bar{\mu}_{i})
.
Together with the preceding steps, this establishes all assertions of the lemma.

We are now ready to prove
Theorem

D.2
.
The proof follows the high-level strategy in
[
47
, Section 2.5.3]
, with several important modifications. For instance,

1.

The properties of the Wasserstein distance force us to use the Borwein–Preiss variational principle instead of the Ekeland variational principle (see
[
47
, Theorem 2.26]
).

2.

The tangent vectors are localized, which renders the ones at different points incomparable.

3.

We cannot resort to the linear structure of the space and the properties of norms.

Proof D.5
.

Proof of
Theorem

D.2
.
Our proof leverages
Lemma

D.3
and the Borwein–Preiss variational principle
[
72
, Theorem 2.5.2]
.
Extremality of
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},\bar{\mu})
provides a neighborhood
U
of
\bar{\mu}
, independent of
\varepsilon
, so that for every
\delta>0
there is a shift
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\|a\right\|<\delta
and
\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\Omega}_{1}\right)\cap\Omega_{2}\cap U=\emptyset
.
Fix
r>0
small enough that
\bar{\mathbb{B}}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};r)\subseteq U
and
\Omega_{i}\cap\bar{\mathbb{B}}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};r)
is closed for
i\in\{1,2\}
, where
\bar{\mathbb{B}}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};r)
denotes the closed Wasserstein ball of radius
r
centered at
\bar{\mu}
.
Clearly, it suffices to prove the claim for
\varepsilon\leq r
.
Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,r]
and apply the above with
\delta=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
, giving
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\left\|a\right\|<\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
.
Let again
W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\coloneqq W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\mu_{1}},\mu_{2}\right)
.
By definition,
W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu})=\left\|a\right\|<\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
.
Since
W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=0
is equivalent to
{(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\mu_{1}}=\mu_{2}
,
\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\Omega}_{1}\right)\cap\Omega_{2}\cap U=\emptyset
is equivalent to
W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})>0
for all
\mu_{1}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\mu_{2}\in\Omega_{2}\cap U
.
We now deploy the Borwein–Preiss variational principle
[
72
, Theorem 2.5.2]
, with their
X=\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
, their
f
being
W_{2}^{a}
, and their
\varepsilon
being
\varepsilon^{2}/8
.
Let us verify its assumptions.
First, since the Wasserstein space
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a complete metric space
[
51
, Theorem 6.18]
, the Cartesian product
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is also a complete metric space (endowed with the product distance). Since
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
are locally closed around
\bar{\mu}
and all statements are local, we can without loss of generality intersect them with
\bar{\mathbb{B}}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};r)
, so that
X=\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
is closed and, being a closed subspace of a complete metric space, complete.

Second,
W_{2}^{a}
is nonnegative and continuous in Wasserstein.
Third, the squared Wasserstein distance (scaled by
\frac{1}{2}
) is a gauge-type function, as defined in
[
72
, Definition 2.5.1]
(see also
[
72
, Theorem 2.5.3]
for an analogous statement in Banach spaces).
Fourth, in the notation of
[
72
, Theorem 2.5.2]
, we consider the sequence
\delta_{j}=2^{-j}
.
Fifth,
(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu})
is an approximate minimizer of
W_{2}^{a}
. Since
W_{2}^{a}\geq 0
and
W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu})=\left\|a\right\|<\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
, we have
W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu})<\inf_{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\in X}W^{a}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
.
By the Borwein–Preiss variational principle, there are probability measures
(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})\in\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
and a sequence of probability measures
((\tilde{\mu}_{1,j},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}))_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}
so that:

•

\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{i},\bar{\mu}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\varepsilon^{2}

and

\frac{1}{2}W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{i},\tilde{\mu}_{i,j}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{2^{-j}}{8}\varepsilon^{2}

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\{1,2\}
;

•

(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})
is a local minimizer of the function
\psi:\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
defined by

\psi(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=W_{2}^{a}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-j}\left(W_{2}\left(\mu_{1},\tilde{\mu}_{1,j}\right)^{2}+W_{2}\left(\mu_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2,j}\right)^{2}\right)
;

•

\psi(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})\leq W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu})<\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
.

Since
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{2},\bar{\mu}\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}<r
, we have
\bar{\mu}_{2}\in\bar{\mathbb{B}}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};r)\subseteq U
. Hence, as above,
W_{2}^{a}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2})>0
, i.e.,
{(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\neq\bar{\mu}_{2}
and
c\coloneqq W_{2}\left({(\mathrm{Id}+a)}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{1}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)>0
.
Thus, the hypotheses of
Lemma

D.3
are met and
Lemma

D.3
, together with
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{i},\bar{\mu}\right)\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}<\varepsilon
by the first bullet, yields the desired statement.

D.2
Product rule

Proposition D.6

(Product rule)
.

1.

For
\Omega_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
and
\Omega_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
\mathcal{C}=\{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\,|\,{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu}\in\Omega_{1},\ {\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu}\in\Omega_{2}\}
and
\bar{\mu}\in\mathcal{C}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}({\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}})\}

\displaystyle\normal_{\mathcal{C}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}({\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}})\}.

In particular, membership of
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}
in the strong regular normal cones implies
\xi\in\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\bar{\mu})
.
If
\Omega_{1}
is
SNC
at
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
and
\Omega_{2}
is
SNC
at
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
, then
\mathcal{C}
is
SNC
at
\bar{\mu}
.
Finally, if
\Omega_{1}=\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}=\{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\,|\,{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu}\in\Omega_{2}\}
, then

\displaystyle\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}=\mathbf{0}_{{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}},{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}})\}

\displaystyle\normal_{\mathcal{C}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}=\mathbf{0}_{{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}},{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}})\},

and
\mathcal{C}
is
SNC
at
\bar{\mu}
whenever
\Omega_{2}
is
SNC
at
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
.

2.

For
\mathcal{C}=\{\mu_{1}\times\delta_{b}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\,|\,\mu_{1}\in\Omega,b\in B\}
and
\bar{\mu}=\bar{\mu}_{1}\times\delta_{\bar{b}}\in\mathcal{C}
, with
\Omega\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\normal}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})}(\bar{\mu})\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu}_{1}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu,\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})\}

\displaystyle\normal_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu}_{1}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu,\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\normal_{B}(\bar{b})\}

\displaystyle\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})}(\bar{\mu})\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu}_{1}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu,\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})\}

\displaystyle\normal_{\mathcal{C}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})

\displaystyle=\{\xi\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}),{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\xi}=\bar{\mu}\,|\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega}^{s}(\bar{\mu}_{1}),{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu,\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\normal_{B}(\bar{b})\}.

In particular, if
B=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]=0
for
\bar{b}>0
and
\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\leq 0
for
\bar{b}=0
.

Proof D.7
.

Proof.
We prove the two parts separately.

1.

Throughout,
\bar{\mu}_{1}\coloneqq{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\bar{\mu}_{2}\coloneqq{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}\in\Omega_{2}
denote the two marginals of
\bar{\mu}
on
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2})
the coordinates of a variation at
\bar{\mu}
.
As a preliminary, notice that
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}=\left\|{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}
for every variation
\xi
at
\bar{\mu}
.

Step 1 (normal-cone inequalities for the marginals).

Let
\bar{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})
; we show that
\left\langle{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{2}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\leq o\left(\left\|\xi_{2}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\right)
for all
\mu_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
and
\xi_{2}\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu}_{2},\mu_{2})}
.
Let
\alpha_{2}\in\Gamma^{1}({\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{2})
attain
\left\langle{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{2}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}
.
Glue
\bar{\xi}
and
\alpha_{2}
along their common marginal
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
, setting the
\mathbb{R}^{m}
-velocity to
0
, to obtain a coupling
\alpha
, and let
\xi
be the variation at
\bar{\mu}
it induces (its
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2})
-marginal).
Then
\upsilon_{1}=0
on the support of
\xi
,
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\xi_{2}
,
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}=\left\|\xi_{2}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}
, and
\alpha\in\Gamma^{1}(\bar{\xi},\xi)
has
(\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2})
-marginal
\alpha_{2}
.
The transport plan generating
\xi
sends
\bar{\mu}
to a target
\mu
with
\mathbb{R}^{m}
-marginal
\bar{\mu}_{1}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-marginal
\mu_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
, so
\mu\in\mathcal{C}
,
\xi
is an admissible test in
Definition

2.24
, and

\displaystyle o\left(\left\|\xi_{2}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\right)=o\left(\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)\geq\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}\geq{}}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}={}}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2})=\left\langle{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{2}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{2}},

where the second equality uses
\upsilon_{1}=0
on the support of
\xi
.
Analogously,
\left\langle{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{1}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\leq o\left(\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\right)
for all
\mu_{1}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\xi_{1}\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1})}
.

Step 2 (the marginals are tangent).

Let
\eta\coloneqq{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
and
\nu_{t}\coloneqq{(\pi_{2}+t\pi_{4})}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
for
t>0
.
We claim that

\lim_{t\searrow 0}\ \frac{W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{2},\nu_{t}\right)}{t}\;=\;\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}.

(D.3)

The inequality “
\leq
” always holds, since the transport plan
{(\pi_{2},\pi_{2}+t\pi_{4})}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
couples
\bar{\mu}_{2}
and
\nu_{t}
with cost
t^{2}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}
; if
\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}=0
(
D.3
) is trivial.
Otherwise, suppose towards a contradiction that
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{2},\nu_{t_{n}}\right)\leq(1-\delta)\,t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}
for some
\delta>0
and
t_{n}\searrow 0
.
Pick
\bar{\alpha}_{n}\in\Gamma_{o}(\nu_{t_{n}},\bar{\mu}_{2})
and glue it to
\bar{\xi}
along
\nu_{t_{n}}={(\pi_{2}+t_{n}\pi_{4})}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
.
This yields
\Theta_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}((\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with coordinates
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z)
, such that

{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}})}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}=\bar{\xi}

and

{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}}+t_{n}\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}},\,\pi_{z})}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}=\bar{\alpha}_{n}.

In particular,
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|z-\bar{x}_{2}-t_{n}\bar{\upsilon}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\Theta_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z)=W_{2}\left(\nu_{t_{n}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)^{2}
.
Define

\xi_{n}\coloneqq{\bigl(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\,0,\,\pi_{z}-\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}}\bigr)}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}},

induced by the transport plan
{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{z})}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}\in\Gamma(\bar{\mu},\mu_{n})
with
\mu_{n}\coloneqq{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{z})}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}
.
Since
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu_{n}}=\bar{\mu}_{1}\in\Omega_{1}
and
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu_{n}}={\pi_{z}}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}=\bar{\mu}_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
, we have
\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
\xi_{n}
is an admissible test in
Definition

2.24
.
Moreover,
{\bigl(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}},\,0,\,\pi_{z}-\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}}\bigr)}_{\#}{\Theta_{n}}\in\Gamma^{1}(\bar{\xi},\xi_{n})
, so, writing
\pi_{z}-\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}}=t_{n}\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}}+(\pi_{z}-\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}}-t_{n}\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}})
and applying Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi_{n}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}

\displaystyle\geq{}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z-\bar{x}_{2}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\Theta_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z)

\displaystyle=t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}+\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z-\bar{x}_{2}-t_{n}\bar{\upsilon}_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}={}}\mathrm{d}\Theta_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},z)

\displaystyle\geq t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}-\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}W_{2}\left(\nu_{t_{n}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\delta\,t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, triangle inequality in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\Theta_{n})
gives
\left\|\xi_{n}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\leq t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}+W_{2}\left(\nu_{t_{n}},\bar{\mu}_{2}\right)\leq 2t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\to 0
.
In particular, observe that
\left\|\xi_{n}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}>0
; otherwise,
z=\bar{x}_{2}

\Theta_{n}\text{-a.e.}
, and we would get
0\geq\delta t_{n}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}^{2}>0
.
Hence
\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi_{n}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\geq\tfrac{\delta}{2}\left\|\eta\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\left\|\xi_{n}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
along a sequence with vanishing norms, contradicting
\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi_{n}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\leq o\left(\left\|\xi_{n}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)
and yielding (
D.3
).
By
[
69
, Theorem 4.19]
, a transport plan with first marginal
\bar{\mu}_{2}
is tangent if and only if it satisfies (
D.3
).
Hence
\eta\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\bar{\mu}_{2})}
and, with Step 1,
\eta\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\bar{\mu}_{2})
.
The same argument shows
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}(\bar{\mu}_{1})}
and thus
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
.
Since
{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}=\bar{\mu}
by
Definition

2.24
, Steps 1–2 prove the inclusion “
\subseteq
” of the first equality.

Step 3 (a lifting lemma).

We show that, if
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\hat{\xi}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}((\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\times(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}))_{\mu}
with
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}({\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu})}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu})}
, then
\hat{\xi}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\mu)}
.
Suppose first that both marginals lie in the respective sets (
2.6
) with
\bar{\varepsilon}_{1},\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}
.
For
\varepsilon\in[0,\min\{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1},\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}\})
their
\varepsilon
-transport plans are optimal, so their supports are cyclically monotone and there are convex
\psi_{\varepsilon}
,
\phi_{\varepsilon}
with
y_{1}+\varepsilon w_{1}\in\partial\psi_{\varepsilon}(y_{1})
and
y_{2}+\varepsilon w_{2}\in\partial\phi_{\varepsilon}(y_{2})
for all
(y_{1},w_{1})
,
(y_{2},w_{2})
in the supports of
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
,
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
(cf.
[
51
, Theorem 5.10]
).
The support of the joint
\varepsilon
-transport plan of
\hat{\xi}
consists of pairs
\bigl((y_{1},y_{2}),(y_{1}+\varepsilon w_{1},y_{2}+\varepsilon w_{2})\bigr)
with
(y_{1},w_{1})
and
(y_{2},w_{2})
in those supports, hence is contained in the graph of
\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}
for the convex, separable
\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z_{1},z_{2})\coloneqq\psi_{\varepsilon}(z_{1})+\phi_{\varepsilon}(z_{2})
; the joint
\varepsilon
-transport plan is therefore optimal, and
\hat{\xi}
lies in the joint set (
2.6
) with
\bar{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\min\{\bar{\varepsilon}_{1},\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}\}
.
Note that only the
supports
of the marginals enter: the correlations between
w_{1}
,
w_{2}
, and the base are arbitrary.
In general, pick
\zeta^{1}_{k}
,
\zeta^{2}_{k}
in the sets (
2.6
) at
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu}
,
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu}
converging to
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
,
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
in the respective distances, and let
\theta^{1}_{k}\in\Gamma^{1}({\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}},\zeta^{1}_{k})
and
\theta^{2}_{k}\in\Gamma^{1}({\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}},\zeta^{2}_{k})
attain the two distances.
Glue
\theta^{1}_{k}
and
\theta^{2}_{k}
to
\hat{\xi}
, along
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
, respectively.
This yields
\Lambda_{k}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}((\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3})
, with coordinates
(y_{1},y_{2},w_{1},w_{2},u_{1},u_{2})
, such that

{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{w_{1}},\pi_{w_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{k}}=\hat{\xi},

{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{w_{1}},\pi_{u_{1}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{k}}=\theta^{1}_{k},

and

{(\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{w_{2}},\pi_{u_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{k}}=\theta^{2}_{k}.

Set
\hat{\zeta}_{k}\coloneqq{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{u_{1}},\pi_{u_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{k}}
.
Its marginals are
{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{u_{1}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{k}}={(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\theta^{1}_{k}}=\zeta^{1}_{k}
and, likewise,
\zeta^{2}_{k}
, so
\hat{\zeta}_{k}
lies in the joint set (
2.6
) by the first part.
Moreover,
\Lambda_{k}\in\Gamma^{1}(\hat{\xi},\hat{\zeta}_{k})
, so

\displaystyle W_{\mu}\left(\hat{\xi},\hat{\zeta}_{k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|(w_{1},w_{2})-(u_{1},u_{2})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}\leq{}}\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{k}(y_{1},y_{2},w_{1},w_{2},u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle=W_{{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu}}\left({\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}},\zeta^{1}_{k}\right)^{2}+W_{{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\mu}}\left({\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}},\zeta^{2}_{k}\right)^{2}\to 0.

Step 4 (converse inclusion for the strong regular normal cone).

Conversely, let
\bar{\xi}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}=\bar{\mu}
,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\bar{\mu}_{2})
.
By
Definition

2.24
, the two marginals belong to
{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})}(\bar{\mu}_{1})}
and
{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\bar{\mu}_{2})}
, so Step 3 gives
\bar{\xi}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\bar{\mu})}
.
Then, for all
\xi\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{3}-\pi_{1},\pi_{4}-\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu},\mu)}
with
\mu\in\mathcal{C}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\alpha\in\Gamma^{1}(\bar{\xi},\xi)}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}={}}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}+\left\langle{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq o\left(\left\|{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\right)+o\left(\left\|{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\right)\leq o\left(\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)

where the first inequality uses that the joint maximum is upper bounded by the sum of the independent maxima, and the second inequality uses
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu}_{1},{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu})}
with
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\mu}\in\Omega_{1}
together with
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
, and the symmetric memberships for the second factor; the last inequality holds because
\left\|{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}},\left\|{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{2}}\leq\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
, so both error terms are
o\left(\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)
along any admissible family with
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\to 0
.
Hence
\bar{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})
.
Together with Steps 1–2, this proves the first equality.

Step 5 (the strong normal cone).

For “
\subseteq
”, let
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\to\bar{\mu}
, and
\hat{\xi}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n})
with
\hat{\xi}_{n}\to\bar{\xi}
, as in
Definition

2.24
; in particular
{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}=\bar{\mu}
.
The projections
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{}
,
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{}
,
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{}
, and
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{}
are
1
-Lipschitz with respect to
W_{2}
.
By the first equality,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}({\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}})
with
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}}\to\bar{\mu}_{1}
and
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\to{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
, whence
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
; symmetrically,
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu}_{2})
.
For “
\supseteq
”, let
\bar{\xi}
satisfy
{\pi_{12}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}=\bar{\mu}
,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
, and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu}_{2})
. Then, there are
\mu_{1,n}\in\Omega_{1}
,
\mu_{1,n}\to\bar{\mu}_{1}
,
\xi^{1}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1,n})
,
\xi^{1}_{n}\to{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
, and
\mu_{2,n}\in\Omega_{2}
,
\mu_{2,n}\to\bar{\mu}_{2}
,
\xi^{2}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2,n})
,
\xi^{2}_{n}\to{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
.
Choose
\beta^{1}_{n}\in\Gamma({\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi^{1}_{n})
, with coordinates
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},y_{1},w_{1})
, and
\beta^{2}_{n}\in\Gamma({\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi^{2}_{n})
, with coordinates
(\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},y_{2},w_{2})
, such that
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{m})^{2}}\left\|y_{1}-\bar{x}_{1}\right\|^{2}+\left\|w_{1}-\bar{\upsilon}_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\beta^{1}_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},y_{1},w_{1})\to 0
and
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{2}}\left\|y_{2}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}+\left\|w_{2}-\bar{\upsilon}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\beta^{2}_{n}(\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},y_{2},w_{2})\to 0
, and glue them to
\bar{\xi}
, along
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
respectively.
This yields
\Lambda_{n}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}((\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{4})
, with coordinates
(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},y_{1},w_{1},y_{2},w_{2})
, such that

{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}=\bar{\xi},{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{1}},\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{w_{1}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}=\beta^{1}_{n},

and

{(\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}},\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{w_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}=\beta^{2}_{n}.

Set

\bar{\mu}_{n}\coloneqq{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{y_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}

and

\hat{\xi}_{n}\coloneqq{(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{w_{1}},\pi_{w_{2}})}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}.

Then: (i)
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{n}}={\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\xi^{1}_{n}}=\mu_{1,n}\in\Omega_{1}
and
{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}_{n}}=\mu_{2,n}\in\Omega_{2}
, so
\bar{\mu}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
, and
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu}\right)^{2}\leq\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{4}}\left\|y_{1}-\bar{x}_{1}\right\|^{2}+\left\|y_{2}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},y_{1},w_{1},y_{2},w_{2})\to 0
; (ii)
{\bigl((\pi_{\bar{x}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{x}_{2}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{1}},\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}_{2}}),(\pi_{y_{1}},\pi_{y_{2}},\pi_{w_{1}},\pi_{w_{2}})\bigr)}_{\#}{\Lambda_{n}}
couples
\bar{\xi}
and
\hat{\xi}_{n}
with cost
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{4}}\left\|y_{1}-\bar{x}_{1}\right\|^{2}+\left\|y_{2}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}+\left\|w_{1}-\bar{\upsilon}_{1}\right\|^{2}+\left\|w_{2}-\bar{\upsilon}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\Lambda_{n}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{\upsilon}_{1},\bar{\upsilon}_{2},y_{1},w_{1},y_{2},w_{2})\to 0
, so
\hat{\xi}_{n}\to\bar{\xi}
; (iii)
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}={(\pi_{3},\pi_{4})}_{\#}{\beta^{1}_{n}}=\xi^{1}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1,n})
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}=\xi^{2}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2,n})
, with
\mu_{1,n}
and
\mu_{2,n}
the marginals of
\bar{\mu}_{n}
, so Step 4, applied at
\bar{\mu}_{n}
, gives
\hat{\xi}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu}_{n})
.
By
Definition

2.24
,
\bar{\xi}\in\normal_{\mathcal{C}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
.

Step 6 (
SNC
).

Let
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\in\mathcal{C}
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\to\bar{\mu}
, and
\hat{\xi}_{n}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n})
with
\hat{\xi}_{n}\rightharpoonup\hat{\xi}
.
We show
\hat{\xi}_{n}\to\hat{\xi}
.
By the first equality at
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}
,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}({\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}})
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}({\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}})
.
Both sequences of marginals converge narrowly (continuous projections), with bases converging in Wasserstein, so, since
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
are
SNC
,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\to{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}_{n}}\to{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\hat{\xi}}
.
Hence, the second moment of
\hat{\xi}_{n}
converges to that of
\hat{\xi}
, and narrow convergence together with convergence of the second moments is equivalent to convergence in Wasserstein
[
51
, Theorem 6.9]
.

Step 7 (the cylinder case
\Omega_{1}=\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
).

By
Lemma

2.25
and
Proposition

2.22
1
, the strong (regular) normal cones of
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
reduce to
\{\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
is
SNC
; substituting
\Omega_{1}=\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
into the general statement gives the final part.

2.

Write
\bar{\mu}_{1}\coloneqq{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
, so that
\bar{\mu}=\bar{\mu}_{1}\times\delta_{\bar{b}}
, and let
\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu\coloneqq{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
.
We first record two structural facts and then prove the two inclusions in the regular case.
SNC
and the statements for the normal cone follow directly from the expressions for the (strong) regular normal cone, on which its proof below focuses.

Here
\mathcal{C}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})
, variations at

\bar{\mu}
live in
\mathcal{P}_{2}((\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}))
,
and the block projections
\pi_{13}
and
\pi_{24}
retain,
respectively, the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-position with its
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-velocity and the

\mathbb{R}
-position with its
\mathbb{R}
-velocity. Under the identification

\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
, the map
\pi_{13}
coincides with

T
of (
2.9
); we therefore apply

Lemma

A.11
with

{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\cdot}
in place of
{T}_{\#}{\cdot}

and with the last velocity component given by the second component of the

\pi_{24}
-block.

Fact 1 (set of optimal transport plans).

Fix
\mu=\mu_{1}\times\delta_{b}\in\mathcal{C}
. By

Lemma

A.11
1
,
with the lemma’s base and target deterministic coordinates instantiated as

\bar{b}
and
b
, a transport plan of
\bar{\mu}=\bar{\mu}_{1}\times\delta_{\bar{b}}
and

\mu=\mu_{1}\times\delta_{b}
has the form

{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3},\pi_{2},\pi_{4})}_{\#}{(\gamma\times\delta_{(\bar{b},b)})}

with
\gamma
a transport plan of
\bar{\mu}_{1}
and
\mu_{1}
, and its cost is

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x^{\prime}-x\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(x,x^{\prime})+\absolutevalue{b-\bar b}^{2}
;
consequently,

\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu},\mu)=\bigl\{{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3},\pi_{2},\pi_{4})}_{\#}{(\gamma\times\delta_{(\bar{b},b)})}\,|\,\gamma\in\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1})\bigr\}.

Fact 2 (
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
is tangent).

By
Lemma

A.11
2

(under the identification above),

{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\bar{\mu}_{1})}

if and only if
\bar{\xi}\in{\tangent_{\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})}(\bar{\mu})}
.

By Fact 1, every variation
\xi\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{3}-\pi_{1},\pi_{4}-\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu},\mu)}
with
\mu=\mu_{1}\times\delta_{b}\in\mathcal{C}
satisfies
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}=\xi_{1}\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma_{o}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1})}
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\xi}=\delta_{(\bar{b},\,b-\bar{b})}
.

Since
\xi
has last velocity component almost everywhere equal to

b-\bar{b}
(Fact 1),
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}=\xi_{1}
,
and
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}=\delta_{\bar{b}}\times\nu

(so that the last velocity component of
\bar{\xi}
is distributed as

\nu
), (
A.1
) (cf.

Lemma

A.11
3
),
with
c=b-\bar{b}
, gives

\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}=\left\langle{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{1}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}+(b-\bar{b})\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right].

Moreover,
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}=W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1}\right)^{2}+\absolutevalue{b-\bar b}^{2}=W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\mu\right)^{2}
:
the first equality is

Lemma

A.11
3

with
c=b-\bar{b}
, using that
\xi_{1}={\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\xi}

is induced by an optimal transport plan, whence

\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}=W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1}\right)
;
the second is the distance split of

Lemma

A.11
1
.

For the “
\subseteq
” inclusion, let
\bar{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{\mu})
.
Taking
b=\bar{b}
yields
\left\langle{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{1}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\leq o\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1}\right)\right)
for all
\mu_{1}\in\Omega
, i.e.,
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
(since it is tangent by Fact 2).
Taking
\mu_{1}=\bar{\mu}_{1}
, so that
\xi_{1}=\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}
, yields
(b-\bar{b})\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\leq o\left(\absolutevalue{b-\bar b}\right)
for all
b\in B
, i.e.,
\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})
(cf.
[
46
, Definition 6.3]
).

For the “
\supseteq
” inclusion, if
{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu}_{1})
and
\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\in\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})
, then
\left\langle{\pi_{13}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}},\xi_{1}\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}_{1}}\leq o\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1}\right)\right)
and
(b-\bar{b})\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]\leq o\left(\absolutevalue{b-\bar b}\right)
.
Thus,
\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\leq o\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu}_{1},\mu_{1}\right)\right)+o\left(\absolutevalue{b-\bar b}\right)\leq o\left(W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\mu\right)\right)
.
The statement for the strong regular normal cone is analogous.
Finally, for
B=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
one has
\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})=\{0\}
if
\bar{b}>0
and
\hat{\normal}_{B}(\bar{b})=(-\infty,0]
if
\bar{b}=0
, giving the stated sign conditions on
\mathbb{E}^{\upsilon\sim\nu}\left[\upsilon\right]
.

Remark D.8

(Regular vs. strong normal cone)
.

In
Proposition

D.6
1
equality holds only for the strong normal cones; for the (regular) normal cone only the inclusion holds.
Indeed, the reverse inclusion tests against variations from
optimal
transport plans, whose
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-marginal need not be an optimal transport plan of
\bar{\mu}_{2}\coloneqq{\pi_{2}}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
, so
{\pi_{24}}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}\in\hat{\normal}_{\Omega_{2}}(\bar{\mu}_{2})
does not suffice.
The strong normal cones test against all transport plans, removing the gap.

D.3
Intersection rule

We now turn our attention to the intersection rule.

Our first lemma converts an
approximate
strong regular normal, a variation satisfying the strong regular normal cone inequality up to an additive
\varepsilon
, into an
exact
strong regular normal at a nearby base point.

Lemma D.9

(From approximate to exact regular normals)
.

Let
\bar{\mu}\in\Omega\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
\Omega
nonempty and locally closed around
\bar{\mu}
. Let
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
\sigma>0
and let
\xi_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})_{\bar{\mu}}
such that, for all
\mu\in\Omega
, one has
\left\langle\xi_{\varepsilon},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+o\left(\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)
for
\xi\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu},\mu)}
.
Then, there is
\tilde{\mu}\in\Omega
so that
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\tilde{\mu}\right)\leq\sigma
and
\xi\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\tilde{\mu})
so that
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
.

Proof D.10
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into steps.
If
\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
, then
\xi\coloneqq\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\bar{\mu})
and
\tilde{\mu}\coloneqq\bar{\mu}
satisfy these bounds, since
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq W_{\bar{\mu}}\left(\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}},\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)=\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
. Hence, throughout the proof we assume
\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}>\varepsilon+\sigma
.

Step 1 (Reduction and epigraphical lift).

We lift the problem to
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{X})
, where
\mathbb{X}\coloneqq\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}
and we read the coordinates
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x,\beta)
as a base point
\bar{x}
, the value
\upsilon_{\varepsilon}
of the candidate normal
\xi_{\varepsilon}
at
\bar{x}
, a moved point
x\in\Omega
, and an epigraphical variable
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\,|\,{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega,\;\beta\geq 0\right\},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\biggl\{\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\,|\,{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\xi_{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\qquad\beta\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)}\biggr\}.

Both sets are nonempty (e.g.,
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}
, where
\bar{\alpha}\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\xi_{\varepsilon}}
corresponds to the optimal transport plan
{(\mathrm{Id},\mathrm{Id})}_{\#}{\bar{\mu}}
of
\bar{\mu}
with itself). They are also locally closed around
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
:
\Lambda_{2}
is closed since
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{}
is
1
-Lipschitz with respect to
W_{2}
and the second-moment functionals defining its
\beta
-bound are continuous in Wasserstein, while
\Lambda_{1}
inherits local closedness from
\Omega
through the
1
-Lipschitz marginal
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{}
and the closedness of
\beta\geq 0
.

Step 2 (Extremality and the extremal principle).

We now show that
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
is a local extremal point of
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
.
By hypothesis,

\limsup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in\Omega,\,\xi\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\bar{\mu},\mu)}\\
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\to 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\left\langle\xi_{\varepsilon},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}}{\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}}\leq\varepsilon.

Thus, there exists a neighborhood
U
of
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
such that, for every
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in U
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\xi_{\varepsilon}
and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega
, the induced variation
\xi\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\alpha}
of
\Omega
at
\bar{\mu}
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\xi_{\varepsilon},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}

(D.4)

\displaystyle\leq(\varepsilon+\sigma)\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)}.

where the first inequality follows from the definition of inner product and the second from the
\varepsilon
-normality of
\xi_{\varepsilon}
, applicable because
\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)
is small for
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in U
and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega
.
We claim
{(\mathrm{Id}+(0,0,0,a))}_{\#}{\Lambda_{1}}\cap\Lambda_{2}\cap U=\emptyset
for all
a>0
, so that
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0})
is extremal in the sense of
Definition

D.1
. Indeed, a point
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}
in this intersection lies in
\Lambda_{2}
, so
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\xi_{\varepsilon}
.
It is also the
(0,0,0,a)
-shift of a point of
\Lambda_{1}
, i.e.,
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta-a}\in\Lambda_{1}
, so
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega
and
\beta-a\geq 0
. Since
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega
, (
D.4
) together with the
\beta
-bound defining
\Lambda_{2}
forces
\beta\leq 0
, contradicting
\beta\geq a>0
.
Therefore,
Theorem

D.2
applied to
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0})
with accuracy
\sigma>0
yields points
\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}\in\Lambda_{i}
with

\sqrt{W_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha},\alpha_{i}\right)^{2}+\absolutevalue{\beta_i}^{2}}<\sigma,\qquad i\in\{1,2\},

(D.5)

and tangent vectors
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
at
\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}
, with
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}\right\|_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}=1
, that are
\sigma
-close to the strong regular normal cone
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{i}}(\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}})
, and a transport plan
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Gamma(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2})
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}\in\Gamma_{o}(\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}},\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
such that

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})=0.

(D.6)

For
i\in\{1,2\}
we fix
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{i}}(\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}})
with
W_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
.

Step 3 (Structure of the lifted normals).

We write the arrow of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
in coordinates as
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i}=(\boldsymbol{w}_{i},\upsilon_{i},\lambda_{i}^{\beta})
, with
\boldsymbol{w}_{i}
the
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
-component,
\upsilon_{i}
the
x
-component, and
\lambda_{i}^{\beta}
the (scalar)
\beta
-component.
We let
\lambda_{i}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}^{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}}\left[\lambda_{i}^{\beta}\right]
.
We record two facts.

Fact 1.

By
Theorem

D.2
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
are induced by a common optimal transport plan
\gamma
and a single fixed shift
\boldsymbol{a}
between
\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}}
and
\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}
.
As these have Dirac
\beta
-marginals,
\gamma
couples the
\beta
-coordinate by
\delta_{(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})}
and
\boldsymbol{a}
has a constant
\beta
-component.
Thus, the
\beta
-component of the arrow of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
is a constant
c_{i}

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}\text{-a.e.}
. Since
W_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
, the
\beta
-arrow
\lambda_{i}^{\beta}
of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
obeys
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta}-c_{i})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})\leq\sigma^{2}
; in particular
\absolutevalue{\lambda_i-c_i}\leq\sigma
and

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta}-\lambda_{i})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta}-c_{i})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})-(\lambda_{i}-c_{i})^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}.

Hence, since
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})=\lambda_{i}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta}-\lambda_{i})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})
, we have
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}(\lambda_{i}^{\beta})^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})=\lambda_{i}^{2}+\rho_{i}
with
0\leq\rho_{i}\leq\sigma^{2}
. So,

\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})+\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})+\lambda_{i}^{2}+\rho_{i}.

(D.7)

Fact 2.

By the structure of
\Lambda_{1}
,
Proposition

D.6
1
gives
\boldsymbol{w}_{1}=0

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\text{-a.e.}
.

Step 4 (Extraction of the
\Omega
-normal).

By the structure of
\Lambda_{1}
,
Proposition

D.6
(
2
for the
\delta_{\beta}
-factor,
1
for the free block) we have
{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\tilde{\mu})
with
\tilde{\mu}\coloneqq{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}}
.

Step 5 (Controlling
\xi_{\varepsilon}
through
\Lambda_{2}
).

We use the normal
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{2}}(\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
to control
\xi_{\varepsilon}
; its base has
x
-coordinate
x_{2}
, its arrow has components
(\boldsymbol{w}_{2},\upsilon_{2},\lambda_{2}^{\beta})
, and
\lambda_{2}=\mathbb{E}^{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}\left[\lambda_{2}^{\beta}\right]
.
Its
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
-component
\boldsymbol{w}_{2}
is small.
Indeed, by (
D.6
), the
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
-component of the arrow of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
equals, in norm along
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
, that of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}
, which is within
\sigma
of
\boldsymbol{w}_{1}
(the
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
-projection is
1
-Lipschitz and
W\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
); as
\boldsymbol{w}_{1}=0
(Fact 2) and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
is likewise within
\sigma
of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
, we get
\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\bigr)^{1/2}\leq 2\sigma
, i.e.,
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\leq 4\sigma^{2}
.

Since
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{2}}(\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
, every coupling
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X})
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
and
{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\Lambda_{2}
satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}},\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\leq o\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\right)^{1/2}\right),

(D.8)

where
\boldsymbol{q}
denotes a generic point of
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
: its three blocks are a base point of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
, the arrow
\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}}
that
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
attaches to it (that is,
\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}
in the coordinates of Step 3), and a displacement
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}
.
We now use (
D.8
) for displacements
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}=(0,0,\upsilon^{\prime},\Delta\beta)
with
\Delta\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.
For such
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}
the arrow component
\boldsymbol{w}_{2}
is paired with the zero displacement on the
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
-coordinates and drops out, so the left-hand side of (
D.8
) equals
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})+\lambda_{2}\,\Delta\beta
.
Testing with
\upsilon^{\prime}=0
and
\Delta\beta\leq 0
(admissible since lowering
\beta
preserves membership in
\Lambda_{2}
) gives
\lambda_{2}\Delta\beta\leq o\left(\absolutevalue{\Delta\beta}\right)
for all
\Delta\beta\leq 0
and so
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
.
Moreover, since
\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}\in\Lambda_{2}
, we have

\beta_{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2})-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2})}.

(D.9)

We distinguish two cases.

Case (a):
\lambda_{2}=0
.

Set
\alpha_{t}^{\prime}\coloneqq{(\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}},\pi_{x_{2}}+t\pi_{\upsilon_{2}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}
, and let

R(t)\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2}+t\upsilon_{2}-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|x_{2}+t\upsilon_{2}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})}.

As
R
has an affine and a square-root term,
R(t)-R(0)=O(t)
.
Set
\beta_{t}^{\prime}\coloneqq\beta_{2}+\bigl(R(t)-R(0)\bigr)
.
Since
\beta_{2}\leq R(0)
by (
D.9
), we have
\beta_{t}^{\prime}\leq R(t)
, so
\alpha_{t}^{\prime}\times\delta_{\beta_{t}^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{2}
for
t\geq 0
. The associated displacement is
(0,0,t\upsilon_{2},\beta_{t}^{\prime}-\beta_{2})
with
\beta_{t}^{\prime}-\beta_{2}=R(t)-R(0)=O(t)
, induced by

\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}^{\prime}\coloneqq{\big((\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}},\pi_{x_{2}},\beta_{2}),(\pi_{\boldsymbol{w}_{2}},\pi_{\upsilon_{2}},\pi_{\lambda_{2}^{\beta}}),(0,0,\,t\pi_{\upsilon_{2}},\,\beta_{t}^{\prime}-\beta_{2})\big)}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}},

which satisfies
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}^{\prime}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
and
{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}^{\prime}}=\alpha_{t}^{\prime}\times\delta_{\beta_{t}^{\prime}}\in\Lambda_{2}
by construction, so (
D.8
) applies to it.
Since
\lambda_{2}=0
, the left-hand side of (
D.8
) equals
t\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})
and its right-hand side is
o\left(t\right)
.
Dividing by
t>0
and letting
t\searrow 0
gives
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})=0
. Then (
D.7
), with
\lambda_{2}=0
,
\rho_{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
, and
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\leq 4\sigma^{2}
, gives
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}^{2}\leq 5\sigma^{2}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
is
\sigma
-close to
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
, this gives
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}\leq(1+\sqrt{5})\,\sigma
, contradicting
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}=1
for
\sigma
sufficiently small.
Thus,
\lambda_{2}>0
.

Case (b):
\lambda_{2}>0
.

Suppose without loss of generality that (
D.9
) holds with equality.
Indeed, if the inequality is strict, choosing the displacement
\upsilon^{\prime}=0
,
\Delta\beta>0
in (
D.8
) yields
\lambda_{2}\leq 0
and so a contradiction.
For
t\geq 0
we perturb the
x
-coordinate of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
along the direction
\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}
by setting
\alpha_{t}\coloneqq{(\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}},\pi_{x_{2}}+t(\lambda_{2}\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}}+\pi_{\upsilon_{2}}))}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}
, and define

\displaystyle\beta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{t}(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{t}(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2}+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|x_{2}+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})}.

Since the inequality (
D.9
) holds with equality, we have
\alpha_{t}\times\delta_{\beta_{t}}\in\Lambda_{2}
.
The associated displacement is
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}=\big(0,0,\,t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}),\,\beta_{t}-\beta_{2}\big)\in\mathbb{X}
, induced by

\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}\coloneqq{\big((\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}},\pi_{x_{2}},\beta_{2}),(\pi_{\boldsymbol{w}_{2}},\pi_{\upsilon_{2}},\pi_{\lambda_{2}^{\beta}}),(0,0,\,t(\lambda_{2}\pi_{\upsilon_{\varepsilon}}+\pi_{\upsilon_{2}}),\,\beta_{t}-\beta_{2})\big)}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}.

By construction,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X})
,
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
, and
{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}}=\alpha_{t}\times\delta_{\beta_{t}}\in\Lambda_{2}
, so (
D.8
) applies to it.
We expand
\beta_{t}
in
t
as
\beta_{t}=I(t)-(\varepsilon+\sigma)N(t)
, where

\displaystyle I(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2}+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})=I(0)+t\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}),

\displaystyle N(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|x_{2}+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\biggr)^{1/2}.

The map
I
is affine in
t
.
The map
w\mapsto\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|w\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\bigr)^{1/2}
is a seminorm, so its triangle inequality applied to the splitting
x_{2}+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})-\bar{x}=(x_{2}-\bar{x})+t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})
gives, for
t\geq 0
,

N(t)\leq N(0)+t\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\biggr)^{1/2}.

As (
D.9
) holds with equality,
\beta_{2}=I(0)-(\varepsilon+\sigma)N(0)
, so
\beta_{t}-\beta_{2}=\bigl(I(t)-I(0)\bigr)-(\varepsilon+\sigma)\bigl(N(t)-N(0)\bigr)
. Substituting and multiplying by
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{2}t\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})

(D.10)

\displaystyle-\lambda_{2}t(\varepsilon+\sigma)\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\biggr)^{1/2}.

Moreover,
\absolutevalue{\beta_t-\beta_2}=O(t)
, since
I
is affine and
N
Lipschitz in
t
.
Hence,

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}(\boldsymbol{q})=t^{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})+\absolutevalue{\beta_t-\beta_2}^{2}=O(t^{2}).

(D.11)

The inner product in (
D.8
) is

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},t(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon})\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}(\boldsymbol{q})=t\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}).

(D.12)

By (
D.12
) and (
D.10
), the left-hand side of (
D.8
) is bounded below by
t\,Q
, where

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})+\lambda_{2}\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{\varepsilon},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})

(D.13)

\displaystyle-\lambda_{2}(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})-\lambda_{2}(\varepsilon+\sigma)\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})}.

The right-hand side of (
D.8
) is
o\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{t}(\boldsymbol{q})\right)^{1/2}\right)=o\left(t\right)
by (
D.11
). Hence,
t\,Q\leq o\left(t\right)
.
We divide by
t>0
and let
t\searrow 0
to get
Q\leq 0
or equivalently

\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\tfrac{\upsilon_{2}}{\lambda_{2}}+\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})}\leq\varepsilon+\sigma.

(D.14)

Thus,
\hat{\xi}_{2}\coloneqq-\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}{(\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{2}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}
satisfies
W_{\bar{\mu}}\left(\xi_{\varepsilon},\hat{\xi}_{2}\right)\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
.

Step 6 (Lower bound on
\lambda_{2}
).

It remains to transfer the bound from
\hat{\xi}_{2}
to
\xi\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}
, which requires a lower bound on
\lambda_{2}
.
By (
D.7
),
\rho_{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
(Fact 1), and
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\leq 4\sigma^{2}
(Step 5),

\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}^{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})+\lambda_{2}^{2}+5\sigma^{2},

while
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}\geq\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}\right\|_{\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}}-\sigma=1-\sigma
. Moreover, applying triangle inequality to (
D.14
), and
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})=\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}
gives

\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})\biggr)^{1/2}\leq\lambda_{2}\bigl(\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon+\sigma\bigr).

Overall,
(1-\sigma)^{2}\leq\lambda_{2}^{2}\bigl(\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon+\sigma\bigr)^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{2}+5\sigma^{2}
, so
\lambda_{2}\geq c\,\sqrt{(1-\sigma)^{2}-5\sigma^{2}}
with
c\coloneqq\bigl(1+(\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon+\sigma)^{2}\bigr)^{-1/2}
.
In particular, for
\sigma\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
(so that
(1-\sigma)^{2}-5\sigma^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}
) we have
\lambda_{2}\geq c/2>0
, so
\lambda_{2}
is bounded away from
0
and
\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\leq\tfrac{2}{c}
.

Step 7 (Closeness of
\xi
to
\xi_{\varepsilon}
).

Recall
\xi\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}
.
Since
{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\tilde{\mu})
(Step 4), the regular normal cone is a cone, and
\lambda_{2}>0
, we have
\xi\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\tilde{\mu})
.
As
\xi
is based at
\tilde{\mu}
while
\hat{\xi}_{2}
and
\xi_{\varepsilon}
are based at
\bar{\mu}
, the triangle inequality gives
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq W_{2}\left(\xi,\hat{\xi}_{2}\right)+W_{2}\left(\hat{\xi}_{2},\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)
.
Since
W_{\bar{\mu}}\left(\hat{\xi}_{2},\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
by (
D.14
), it remains to bound
W_{2}\left(\xi,\hat{\xi}_{2}\right)
.

Glue the transport plan
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
with the two transport plans realizing the
\sigma
-closeness of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
to
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
, along their shared
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}
- and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
-marginals.
This yields a transport plan
\theta\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{S})
,
\mathbb{S}\coloneqq(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X})^{4}
, that carries
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
.
We write a generic point of
\mathbb{S}
as
