(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})
:
each entry is a pair (base point, arrow) in

\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
, and under
\theta
the four pairs have
laws
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}
,
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}
,

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
, and
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
,
respectively. Since the
\sigma
-closeness of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
to

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
is measured in the Wasserstein
distance at their common base
\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}
,
which couples variations with a common base point,
\boldsymbol{q}_{i}
and

\boldsymbol{q}_{i}^{\parallel}
share their base
\theta\text{-a.e.}
. The
integrands below use only some components of these pairs, in the coordinates
of Step 1 for the bases and of Step 3 for the arrows:
x_{1}
is the

x
-coordinate (the moved point) of the common base of

\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel}
and
\boldsymbol{q}_{1}
, whereas
\bar{x}_{2}
and

x_{2}
are the
\bar{x}
- and
x
-coordinates of the common base of

\boldsymbol{q}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel}
; finally,

\upsilon_{i}
and
\upsilon_{i}^{\parallel}
are the

x
-components of the arrows of
\boldsymbol{q}_{i}
and

\boldsymbol{q}_{i}^{\parallel}
, respectively.
Under
\theta
,
\xi
has base point
x_{1}\sim\tilde{\mu}
and arrow
\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\upsilon_{1}^{\parallel}
, whereas
\hat{\xi}_{2}=-\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}{(\pi_{\bar{x}},\pi_{\upsilon_{2}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}
has base point
\bar{x}_{2}\sim\bar{\mu}
and arrow
-\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\upsilon_{2}^{\parallel}
.
Therefore,
\theta
induces a coupling of
\xi
and
\hat{\xi}_{2}
and so

W_{2}\left(\xi,\hat{\xi}_{2}\right)^{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|x_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})+\tfrac{1}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\parallel}+\upsilon_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel}).

We bound the two integrals separately. For the arrows, the triangle inequality in
L^{2}(\mathbb{S};\theta)
together with
\upsilon_{1}=-\upsilon_{2}
(see (
D.6
)) gives

\displaystyle\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\parallel}+\upsilon_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}^{\parallel}-\upsilon_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}-\upsilon_{2}^{\parallel}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq 2\sigma,

where the first and third terms are each at most
\sigma
by the
\sigma
-closeness of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
to
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
and the middle one vanishes. For the base points, the triangle inequality gives

\displaystyle\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|x_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|x_{1}-x_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|x_{2}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\Bigr)^{1/2},

where
x_{2}
is the moved point of
\alpha_{2}
. The first term is at most
2\sigma
, as, under
\theta
,
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are coupled by the optimal transport plan between
\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}}
and
\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}
, and these two points are each within
\sigma
of
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
by (
D.5
).
The second term equals
\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2})\bigr)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\sigma
, since
(\bar{x},\upsilon_{\varepsilon},x_{2})\mapsto x_{2}-\bar{x}
is
\sqrt{2}
-Lipschitz,
x_{2}=\bar{x}

\bar{\alpha}\text{-a.e.}
, and
W_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha},\alpha_{2}\right)<\sigma
by (
D.5
). Hence
\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{S}}\left\|x_{1}-\bar{x}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\theta(\boldsymbol{q}_{1}^{\parallel},\boldsymbol{q}_{1},\boldsymbol{q}_{2},\boldsymbol{q}_{2}^{\parallel})\bigr)^{1/2}\leq(2+\sqrt{2})\,\sigma
.
Combining the two bounds and using
\lambda_{2}\geq c/2
(Step 6) gives

W_{2}\left(\xi,\hat{\xi}_{2}\right)\leq(2+\sqrt{2})\,\sigma+\frac{2\sigma}{\lambda_{2}}\leq\Bigl(2+\sqrt{2}+\tfrac{4}{c}\Bigr)\sigma.

Overall,
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq(\varepsilon+\sigma)+\bigl(2+\sqrt{2}+\tfrac{4}{c}\bigr)\sigma=\varepsilon+C\sigma
with
C\coloneqq 3+\sqrt{2}+\tfrac{4}{c}
.

Step 8 (Conclusion).

Since
\pi_{3}
is
1
-Lipschitz and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\bar{\alpha}}=\bar{\mu}
, (
D.5
) gives
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\tilde{\mu}\right)\leq W_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha},\alpha_{1}\right)<\sigma
. We have thus produced, at the accuracy
\sigma
,
\tilde{\mu}\in\Omega
and
\xi\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega}(\tilde{\mu})
with
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\tilde{\mu}\right)<\sigma
and
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\varepsilon+C\sigma
. For
\sigma\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
we have
c\geq c_{0}\coloneqq\bigl(1+(\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon+\tfrac{1}{4})^{2}\bigr)^{-1/2}
, hence
C\leq C_{0}\coloneqq 3+\sqrt{2}+4/c_{0}
; the constant
C_{0}
is larger than
1
and depends only on
\left\|\xi_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
and
\varepsilon
. Given the target
\sigma>0
of the statement, running the construction at accuracy
s\coloneqq\min\{\sigma/C_{0},\tfrac{1}{4}\}\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
in place of
\sigma
then yields
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\tilde{\mu}\right)<s\leq\sigma
and
W_{2}\left(\xi,\xi_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\varepsilon+C_{0}s\leq\varepsilon+\sigma
, which proves the lemma.

Our second lemma provides us with a fuzzy intersection rule, which will be key in establishing the general intersection rule below.

Lemma D.11

(A fuzzy intersection rule)
.

Let
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be arbitrary sets locally closed around
\bar{\mu}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
, and let
\bar{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}(\bar{\mu})
.
Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there are
\lambda\geq 0
,
\mu_{i}\in\Omega_{i}\cap\mathbb{B}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};\varepsilon)
,
\mu_{2}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}\cap\mathbb{B}_{W_{2}}(\bar{\mu};2\varepsilon)
, variations
\xi_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})_{\mu_{1}}
and
\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})_{\mu_{2}}
that are
\varepsilon
-close with respect to
W_{2}
to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1})
and
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2}^{\prime})
respectively, and
\gamma\in\Gamma(\bar{\xi},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
so that

\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq\varepsilon^{2}
,

\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq\varepsilon^{2}
,
and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-(\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})=0.

(D.15)

Moreover,

\max\{\lambda,\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\}=1.

(D.16)

Proof D.12
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into steps.
We mirror the proof of
Lemma

D.9
, applying the extremal principle in
\mathbb{X}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Step 1 (Epigraphical lift).

As in
Lemma

D.9
, we read the coordinates
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x,\beta)
of
\mathbb{X}
as a base point
\bar{x}
, the value
\bar{\upsilon}
of the candidate normal
\bar{\xi}
at
\bar{x}
, a moved point
x
, and an epigraphical variable
\beta
. For
\alpha\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
we define

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Bigl\{\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\,|\,{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{1},\;\beta\geq 0\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Bigl\{\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R})\,|\,{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\bar{\xi},\;{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{2},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\beta\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)-\varepsilon\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)}\Bigr\}.

Both sets are nonempty. Indeed,
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}\in\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}
, where
\bar{\alpha}\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\bar{\xi}}
corresponds to the moved point coinciding with the base point
\bar{\mu}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
.
They are also locally closed around
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
, by the same continuity and closedness arguments as in
Lemma

D.9
:
\Lambda_{2}
is closed since
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{}
is
1
-Lipschitz with respect to
W_{2}
and the expected value is continuous in Wasserstein, while
\Lambda_{1}
inherits local closedness from
\Omega_{1}
.

Step 2 (Extremality and the extremal principle).

We claim that
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
is a local extremal point of
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
. Since
\bar{\xi}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}(\bar{\mu})
, there is a neighborhood
U
of
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
such that every
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in U
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\bar{\xi}
and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
, with induced variation
\xi\coloneqq{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\alpha}
of
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
at
\bar{\mu}
, satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)\leq\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}=\varepsilon\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)},

(D.17)

the first inequality since
\alpha
is one admissible coupling of
\bar{\xi}
and
\xi
, and the second by the strong regular normal cone inequality, which gives
\left\langle\bar{\xi},\xi\right\rangle_{\bar{\mu}}\leq o\left(\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\right)\leq\varepsilon\left\|\xi\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
on
U
small enough.
For any
a>0
, a point
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta}\in{(\mathrm{Id}+(0,0,0,a))}_{\#}{\Lambda_{1}}\cap\Lambda_{2}\cap U
lies in
\Lambda_{2}
(so
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\alpha}=\bar{\xi}
and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{2}
) and is the
(0,0,0,a)
-shift of a point of
\Lambda_{1}
(i.e.,
\alpha\times\delta_{\beta-a}\in\Lambda_{1}
, so
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\beta-a\geq 0
).
Then
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
, so (
D.17
) applies and the definition of
\Lambda_{2}
forces
\beta\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)-\varepsilon\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)}\leq 0
. Thus,
\beta\leq 0
, contradicting
\beta\geq a>0
. Hence,
{(\mathrm{Id}+(0,0,0,a))}_{\#}{\Lambda_{1}}\cap\Lambda_{2}\cap U=\emptyset
for all
a>0
, and
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
is extremal in the sense of
Definition

D.1
.
Therefore,
Theorem

D.2
, applied to
(\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2},\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0})
at accuracy
\sigma
, yields points
\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}\in\Lambda_{i}
with

\sqrt{W_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha},\alpha_{i}\right)^{2}+\absolutevalue{\beta_i}^{2}}<\sigma,\qquad i\in\{1,2\},

(D.18)

variations
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
at
\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}
(with
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}\right\|_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}=1
) that are
\sigma
-close to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{i}}(\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}})
, and a transport plan
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\Gamma(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2})
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}\in\Gamma_{o}(\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}},\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
such that

\int_{(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X})^{2}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2})=0.

(D.19)

We fix
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{i}}(\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}})
with
W_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
, and set
\mu_{1}\coloneqq{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha_{1}}\in\Omega_{1}
and
\mu_{2}\coloneqq{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\alpha_{2}}\in\Omega_{2}
.

Step 3 (Structure of the lifted normals).

We write the arrow of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
in coordinates as
\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i}=(\boldsymbol{w}_{i},\upsilon_{i},\lambda_{i}^{\beta})
, with
\boldsymbol{w}_{i}
the
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon})
-component,
\upsilon_{i}
the
x
-component, and
\lambda_{i}^{\beta}
the (scalar)
\beta
-component; we denote by
x_{i}
the moved-point coordinate of the base of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
, set
\lambda_{i}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}^{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}}\left[\lambda_{i}^{\beta}\right]
.
We now recall Facts 1 and 2 of the proof of
Lemma

D.9
(with
\bar{\upsilon}
playing the role of
\upsilon_{\varepsilon}
). First, by
Theorem

D.2
the
\beta
-arrow of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}
is
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}\text{-a.e.}
a constant
c_{i}
, and
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{1}=-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{2}
gives
c_{1}=-c_{2}
. Consequently, the
\beta
-arrow
\lambda_{i}^{\beta}
of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}
is
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\text{-a.e.}
within
\sigma
of its mean
\lambda_{i}
(and
\absolutevalue{\lambda_i-c_i}\leq\sigma
), which yields the norm decomposition

\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\alpha_{i}\times\delta_{\beta_{i}}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{w}_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})+\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}^{\parallel}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{i})+\lambda_{i}^{2}+\rho_{i},\qquad 0\leq\rho_{i}\leq\sigma^{2}.

(D.20)

Second, by the structure of
\Lambda_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{w}_{1}=0

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\text{-a.e.}
.

Step 4 (Normal to
\Omega_{1}
).

By the structure of
\Lambda_{1}
,
Proposition

D.6
(
2
for the
\delta_{\beta}
-factor,
1
for the free block) we have
{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1})
and
\lambda_{1}\leq 0
.

Step 5 (Normal to
\Omega_{2}
).

We extract from
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
a normal to
\Omega_{2}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Lambda_{2}}(\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
, every coupling
\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X})
with
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
and
{(\pi_{1}+\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in\Lambda_{2}
satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}},\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\leq o\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\right)^{1/2}\right),

(D.21)

where
\boldsymbol{q}
denotes a generic point of
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
: its three blocks are a base point of
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
, the arrow
\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}}
that
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}
attaches to it (that is,
\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}}=\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{2}
in the coordinates of Step 3), and a displacement
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}
.
We will use it only for displacements
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}=(0,0,\upsilon^{\prime},\Delta\beta)
, for which it holds
\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\bar{\upsilon}},\boldsymbol{\upsilon}^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})=\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2},\upsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})+\lambda_{2}\,\Delta\beta
.
Testing
\upsilon^{\prime}=0
with a constant
\Delta\beta\leq 0
, admissible since lowering
\beta
preserves membership in
\Lambda_{2}
, gives
\lambda_{2}\Delta\beta\leq o\left(\absolutevalue{\Delta\beta}\right)
for all
\Delta\beta\leq 0
, whence
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
. Next, fix
\mu^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}
and a variation
\xi^{\prime}\in{(\pi_{1},\pi_{2}-\pi_{1})}_{\#}{\Gamma(\mu_{2},\mu^{\prime})}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
be a coupling as above whose displacement has
x
-component
\upsilon^{\prime}
realizing
\xi^{\prime}
. Define

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x_{2}+\upsilon^{\prime}-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})-\varepsilon\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|x_{2}+\upsilon^{\prime}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\Bigr)^{1/2},

(D.22)

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})-\varepsilon\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{2})-\varepsilon\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x_{2}-\bar{x}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\alpha_{2}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{2})\Bigr)^{1/2},

where the second line uses
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}
and is precisely the bound on
\beta
in the definition of
\Lambda_{2}
at
\alpha_{2}
.
Hence,
\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}\in\Lambda_{2}
implies
\beta_{2}\leq\beta_{0}^{\prime}
.
Consider the displacement
(0,0,\upsilon^{\prime},\Delta\beta)
with
\Delta\beta\coloneqq\beta^{\prime}-\beta_{0}^{\prime}
, which moves the
x
-marginal to
\mu^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}
and sets the epigraphical variable to
\beta_{2}+\Delta\beta=\beta_{2}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta_{0}^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}
(as
\beta_{2}\leq\beta_{0}^{\prime}
), so the moved configuration lies in
\Lambda_{2}
.
The triangle inequality for the seminorm
w\mapsto(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|w\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q}))^{1/2}
applied to
x_{2}+\upsilon^{\prime}-\bar{x}=(x_{2}-\bar{x})+\upsilon^{\prime}
, together with the Cauchy–Schwarz bound
\absolutevalue{\int_{\epigraphSpace\times\epigraphSpace\times\epigraphSpace}\innerProduct{\bar\varTangentReals}{\varTangentReals'}\d\boldsymbol\varTangentCoupling(\boldsymbol{q})}\leq\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}
, gives

\Delta\beta\geq\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})-\varepsilon\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\Bigr)^{1/2},\qquad\absolutevalue{\Delta\beta}\leq(\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon)\,\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}.

Substituting into (
D.21
), whose right-hand side is
o\left(\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}\right)
(since
(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})+\absolutevalue{\Delta\beta}^{2})^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{1+(\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}+\varepsilon)^{2}}\,\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}
by the bound on
\absolutevalue{\Delta\beta}
), and using
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
,

\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\langle\upsilon_{2}+\lambda_{2}\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon^{\prime}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\leq\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}\left\|\upsilon^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{q})\Bigr)^{1/2}+o\left(\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}\right).

(D.23)

As
\mu^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}
and the coupling
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
realizing
\xi^{\prime}
were arbitrary, (
D.23
) yields
\left\langle\eta_{2}^{\parallel},\xi^{\prime}\right\rangle_{\mu_{2}}\leq\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}+o\left(\left\|\xi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mu_{2}}\right)
for every
\mu^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}
, where
\eta_{2}^{\parallel}\coloneqq{(\pi_{x_{2}},\,\pi_{\upsilon_{2}}+\lambda_{2}\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}^{\parallel}}
.
Since
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\eta_{2}^{\parallel}}=\mu_{2}
, the variation
\eta_{2}^{\parallel}
satisfies the hypothesis of
Lemma

D.9
at
\mu_{2}
with
\lambda_{2}\varepsilon
in place of
\varepsilon
.
Thus, by
Lemma

D.9
, with accuracies
\lambda_{2}\varepsilon
and
\sigma
, there are
\tilde{\mu}_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
with
W_{2}\left(\mu_{2},\tilde{\mu}_{2}\right)\leq\sigma
and a strong regular normal
\bar{\eta}_{2}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{2})
with
W_{2}\left(\eta_{2}^{\parallel},\bar{\eta}_{2}\right)\leq\lambda_{2}\varepsilon+\sigma
.

Step 6 (Cancellation and the multiplier identity).

By (
D.19
),
\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{1}=-\boldsymbol{\upsilon}_{2}

\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\text{-a.e.}
. Let

\eta_{1}\coloneqq{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}},\qquad\eta_{2}\coloneqq{(\pi_{x_{2}},\,\pi_{\upsilon_{2}}+\lambda_{2}\pi_{\bar{\upsilon}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}},\qquad\lambda\coloneqq\lambda_{2}\geq 0.

Pushing
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
forward under the map that extracts the pair
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon})
from the
\Lambda_{2}
-factor, the pair
(x_{1},\upsilon_{1})
from
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}
, and the pair
(x_{2},\upsilon_{2}+\lambda\bar{\upsilon})
from
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{2}
yields a transport plan
\gamma\in\Gamma(\bar{\xi},\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
. Along
\gamma
the arrow of
\eta_{1}
is
\upsilon_{1}
, that of
\eta_{2}
is
\upsilon_{2}+\lambda\bar{\upsilon}
, and
\upsilon_{1}=-\upsilon_{2}
, so
\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-(\upsilon_{1}+(\upsilon_{2}+\lambda\bar{\upsilon}))=0
holds
\gamma\text{-a.e.}
.

Step 7 (Closeness to the strong regular normal cones).

The extracted normals are close to the strong regular normal cones. First,
\eta_{1}
is
\sigma
-close to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1})
: by Step 4,
{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1})
, the projection
(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})
is
1
-Lipschitz, and
W_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
, so
W_{2}\left(\eta_{1},{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}\right)\leq\sigma
. Second, the same Lipschitz bound gives
W_{2}\left(\eta_{2},\eta_{2}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
, so by Step 5
\eta_{2}
is
(\lambda_{2}\varepsilon+2\sigma)
-close to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{2})
.

Step 8 (Nontriviality).

We show that
m\coloneqq\max\{\lambda,\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\}
is bounded away from
0
for
\sigma
small. We proceed in three steps.
First, we bound the projected normal
\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\coloneqq{(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})}_{\#}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}}
. From
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}\right\|=1
and
W_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
we get
(1-\sigma)^{2}\leq\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|^{2}\leq(1+\sigma)^{2}
. Since
\boldsymbol{w}_{1}=0
(Fact 2) and
0\leq\rho_{1}\leq\sigma^{2}
, the decomposition (
D.20
) reads
\left\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|^{2}=\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}+\rho_{1}
, whence
\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}\geq(1-\sigma)^{2}-\sigma^{2}=1-2\sigma
.
Second, we transfer this bound from
\eta_{1}^{\parallel}
to
\eta_{1}
. The projection
(\pi_{x_{1}},\pi_{\upsilon_{1}})
is
1
-Lipschitz, so
W_{2}\left(\eta_{1},\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right)\leq\sigma
by Step 7, and hence
\bigl|\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}-\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\bigr|\leq\sigma
by the reverse triangle inequality. With
\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\leq 1+\sigma
this also gives
\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\leq\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}+\sigma\leq 1+2\sigma
. The identity
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a-b)(a+b)
then yields
\bigl|\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}-\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}\bigr|\leq\bigl|\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}-\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\bigr|\bigl(\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}+\left\|\eta_{1}^{\parallel}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\bigr)\leq\sigma(2+3\sigma)=2\sigma+3\sigma^{2}
, and hence
\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}+\lambda_{1}^{2}\geq(1-2\sigma)-(2\sigma+3\sigma^{2})=1-4\sigma-3\sigma^{2}
.
Third, we compare the two multipliers, as the bound just obtained involves
\lambda_{1}
and not
\lambda=\lambda_{2}
. By Fact 1,
c_{1}=-c_{2}
and
\absolutevalue{\lambda_i-c_i}\leq\sigma
, so
\absolutevalue{\lambda_1+\lambda_2}\leq 2\sigma
. Since
\lambda_{1}\leq 0
(Step 4) and
\lambda_{2}\geq 0
(Step 5), we have
\absolutevalue{\lambda_1}=-\lambda_{1}
, hence
\bigl|\lambda-\absolutevalue{\lambda_1}\bigr|=\absolutevalue{\lambda_2+\lambda_1}\leq 2\sigma
and in particular
\lambda\geq\absolutevalue{\lambda_1}-2\sigma
.
Combining the three estimates,
m\geq\max\{\absolutevalue{\lambda_1},\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\}-2\sigma\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\lambda_{1}^{2}+\left\|\eta_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}^{2}}-2\sigma\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{1-4\sigma-3\sigma^{2}}-2\sigma
, which is bounded away from
0
for
\sigma
small.

Step 9 (Conclusion).

Renormalizing by the positive factor
m^{-1}
, which preserves the cones and scales the multiplier identity of Step 6, we set
\xi_{1}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{m}\eta_{1}
and
\xi_{2}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{m}\eta_{2}
and replace
\lambda
by
\lambda/m
. Then
\max\{\lambda,\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\mu_{1}}\}=1
, which is (
D.16
), and the rescaled transport plan
\gamma\in\Gamma(\bar{\xi},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
still satisfies
\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-(\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2})=0

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
, which gives (
D.15
).

Let
\mu_{2}^{\prime}\coloneqq\tilde{\mu}_{2}\in\Omega_{2}
be the base of the exact
\Omega_{2}
-normal of Step 5. By Step 7,
\xi_{1}
is
\tfrac{\sigma}{m}
-close to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1})
and
\xi_{2}
is
\tfrac{1}{m}(\lambda_{2}\varepsilon+2\sigma)
-close to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2}^{\prime})
. Since
\pi_{3}
is
1
-Lipschitz and
{\pi_{3}}_{\#}{\bar{\alpha}}=\bar{\mu}
, estimate (
D.18
) gives
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\mu_{i}\right)<\sigma
for
i\in\{1,2\}
; with
W_{2}\left(\mu_{2},\mu_{2}^{\prime}\right)\leq\sigma
(Step 5) this also gives
W_{2}\left(\bar{\mu},\mu_{2}^{\prime}\right)<2\sigma
.

We bound the base displacements of
\gamma
. Under
\gamma
,
\bar{x}
and
x_{2}
are the base point and the moved point of
\alpha_{2}
(Step 6), so their joint law is the
(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})
-marginal of
\alpha_{2}
. Since
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x)\mapsto\bar{x}-x
is
\sqrt{2}
-Lipschitz,
\bar{x}=x
on the support of
\bar{\alpha}
, and
W_{2}\left(\bar{\alpha},\alpha_{2}\right)<\sigma
by (
D.18
), we get
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq 2\sigma^{2}
. Next,
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are coupled under
\gamma
by the optimal transport plan
{(\pi_{1},\pi_{3})}_{\#}{\gamma_{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}\in\Gamma_{o}(\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}},\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}})
; since
\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}}
and
\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}
are each within
\sigma
of
\bar{\alpha}\times\delta_{0}
by (
D.18
), this gives
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|x_{1}-x_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq W_{2}\left(\alpha_{1}\times\delta_{\beta_{1}},\alpha_{2}\times\delta_{\beta_{2}}\right)^{2}\leq(2\sigma)^{2}
. The triangle inequality then gives
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq(\sqrt{2}+2)^{2}\sigma^{2}
.
Finally, all error terms are bounded by an absolute constant
C\geq 1
times
\varepsilon+\sigma
or
\sigma^{2}
.
The construction with
\varepsilon/(2C)
in place of
\varepsilon
and
\sigma=\varepsilon/(2C)
thus proves the lemma.

We are now ready to state and prove the intersection rule for the strong normal cone.

Theorem D.13

(Intersection rule for strong normal cones)
.

Let
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
be locally closed sets around
\bar{\mu}\in\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
.
Suppose that (i)

\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\cap\left(-\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\right)=\{\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}}\}

and that (ii) either
\Omega_{1}
or
\Omega_{2}
is
SNC
at
\bar{\mu}
. Then,

\normal_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\subseteq\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})+\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
.

Proof D.14
.

Proof.
We divide the proof into steps.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
\Omega_{1}
is
SNC
at
\bar{\mu}
.

Step 1 (Approximation and per-step fuzzy rule).

Let
\bar{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
. By
Definition

2.24
, there are
\mu_{k}\to\bar{\mu}
in
\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}
and strong regular normals
\xi_{k}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{k})
such that
\xi_{k}\to\bar{\xi}
. As
\xi_{k}
is a strong regular normal, for a sequence
\varepsilon_{k}\searrow 0
,
Lemma

D.11
applies to it at
\mu_{k}
(in particular, since
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
are locally closed around
\bar{\mu}
, they are locally closed around
\mu_{k}
for
k
large enough) with accuracy
\varepsilon_{k}
, providing a multiplier
\lambda_{k}\geq 0
, base points
\mu_{i,k}\in\Omega_{i}\cap\mathbb{B}_{W_{2}}(\mu_{k};\varepsilon_{k})
, a point
\mu_{2,k}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{2}\cap\mathbb{B}_{W_{2}}(\mu_{k};2\varepsilon_{k})
, variations
\xi_{1,k},\xi_{2,k}
that are
\varepsilon_{k}
-close with respect to
W_{2}
to
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1,k})
and
\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2,k}^{\prime})
respectively, and a transport plan
\gamma_{k}\in\Gamma(\xi_{k},\xi_{1,k},\xi_{2,k})
with
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq\varepsilon_{k}^{2}
and
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{2}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\leq\varepsilon_{k}^{2}
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\lambda_{k}\bar{\upsilon}-(\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=0

(D.24)

\displaystyle\max\{\lambda_{k},\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}\}

\displaystyle=1.

(D.25)

Since
W_{2}\left(\mu_{i,k},\bar{\mu}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{k}+W_{2}\left(\mu_{k},\bar{\mu}\right)
, we have
\mu_{i,k}\to\bar{\mu}
.

Step 2 (Boundedness and compactness).

We first record uniform bounds on the multipliers
\lambda_{k}
and on the variation norms. Since
\xi_{k}\to\bar{\xi}
, the norms
\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}
converge to
\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
and are in particular bounded, while (
D.25
) gives
\lambda_{k}\leq 1
and
\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}\leq 1
. For the remaining norm, (
D.24
) gives
\upsilon_{2}=\lambda_{k}\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1}

\gamma_{k}\text{-a.e.}
. The triangle inequality then yields
\left\|\xi_{2,k}\right\|_{\mu_{2,k}}\leq\lambda_{k}\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}+\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}
, which is bounded as well.
The marginals
\mu_{k},\mu_{1,k},\mu_{2,k}
converge to
\bar{\mu}
and are hence tight.
Moreover,
\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}^{2}
,
\left\|\xi_{i,k}\right\|_{\mu_{i,k}}^{2}
are uniformly bounded, hence the second marginals are tight. By Prokhorov’s theorem
[
35
, Theorem 5.1.3]
and
[
51
, Lemma 4.4]
, along a subsequence we have
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda\geq 0
,
\xi_{i,k}\rightharpoonup\xi_{i}
for some
\xi_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\gamma_{k}\rightharpoonup\gamma\in\Gamma(\bar{\xi},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
.
The first marginal of
\xi_{i}
is
\bar{\mu}
. Indeed
{\pi_{1}}_{\#}{\xi_{i,k}}=\mu_{i,k}\to\bar{\mu}
, and the first marginal is narrowly continuous.
It remains to show that
\bar{x}=x_{1}=x_{2}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
.
Narrow convergence of
\gamma_{k}\rightharpoonup\gamma
together with lower semicontinuity gives

\displaystyle\!\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\bar{x}-x_{i}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\varepsilon_{k}^{2}=0.

Therefore,
\bar{x}=x_{1}=x_{2}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
.

Step 3 (Normal to
\Omega_{1}
via sequential normal compactness).

Pick
\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1,k})
with
W_{2}\left(\xi_{1,k},\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{k}
; then
\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\rightharpoonup\xi_{1}
. Since
\Omega_{1}
is
SNC
and
\mu_{1,k}\to\bar{\mu}
,
Definition

2.27
gives
\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\to\xi_{1}
, whence
\xi_{1,k}\to\xi_{1}
and, in particular,
\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}\to\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
. As
\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mu_{1,k})
,
\mu_{1,k}\to\bar{\mu}
, and
\hat{\xi}_{1,k}\to\xi_{1}
,
Definition

2.24
gives
\xi_{1}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
.

Step 4 (Multiplier identity in the limit).

Eq. (
D.24
) states that
\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2}=\lambda_{k}\bar{\upsilon}
holds
\gamma_{k}\text{-a.e.}
. Thus,
\upsilon_{2}-(\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1})=(\lambda_{k}-\lambda)\bar{\upsilon}

\gamma_{k}\text{-a.e.}
.
Since the
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon})
-marginal of
\gamma_{k}
is
\xi_{k}
we have

\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}-(\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})=(\lambda_{k}-\lambda)^{2}\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}^{2}\to 0,

where we used that
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda
and
\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}
is bounded.
Again by narrow lower semicontinuity we have

\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\upsilon_{2}-(\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})=0.

Hence,
\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2}=\lambda\bar{\upsilon}
holds
\gamma\text{-a.e.}
. Moreover,
\bar{x}=x_{1}=x_{2}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
(Step 2), so
\gamma
couples
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
over a common first marginal. By definition,
\xi_{1}\,+_{\gamma}\,\xi_{2}
is the law of
(x_{1},\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2})
under
\gamma
.
Since
\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2}=\lambda\bar{\upsilon}
and
x_{1}=\bar{x}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
, this is the law of
(\bar{x},\lambda\bar{\upsilon})
, that is
\lambda\bar{\xi}
. Thus,

\lambda\bar{\xi}=\xi_{1}\,+_{\gamma}\,\xi_{2}.

(D.26)

Step 5 (Normal to
\Omega_{2}
via the cancellation).

Expanding the square and using
\upsilon_{2}=\lambda_{k}\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1}
,

\left\|\xi_{2,k}\right\|_{\mu_{2,k}}^{2}=\lambda_{k}^{2}\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}^{2}-2\lambda_{k}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})+\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}^{2}.

We show that the cross term converges. We work with the
(\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon_{1})
-marginal
m_{k}\coloneqq{(\pi_{2},\pi_{4})}_{\#}{\gamma_{k}}
of
\gamma_{k}
.
Clearly,
m_{k}
converges narrowly to the corresponding marginal
m
of
\gamma
(as
\gamma_{k}\rightharpoonup\gamma
), and its second moment
\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}^{2}+\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}^{2}
converges to
\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}+\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}
(as
\xi_{k}\to\bar{\xi}
and
\xi_{1,k}\to\xi_{1}
), which equals the second moment of
m
. By
[
51
, Theorem 6.9]
, narrow convergence of
m_{k}
together with convergence of its second moment is equivalent to
m_{k}\to m
.
Thus,
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})\to\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})
.
Passing to the limit and using
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda
,
\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|_{\mu_{k}}\to\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
,
\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}\to\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
, and the convergence of the cross term established above, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left\|\xi_{2,k}\right\|_{\mu_{2,k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\left\|\bar{\xi}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}-2\lambda\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\langle\bar{\upsilon},\upsilon_{1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})+\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})^{3}}\left\|\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon},x_{1},\upsilon_{1},x_{2},\upsilon_{2})=\left\|\xi_{2}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}^{2},

where the second line recombines the square (the
(\bar{x},\bar{\upsilon})
- and
(x_{1},\upsilon_{1})
-marginals of
\gamma
being
\bar{\xi}
and
\xi_{1}
, respectively) and applies (
D.26
) (i.e.,
\upsilon_{2}=\lambda\bar{\upsilon}-\upsilon_{1}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
).
Thus,
\left\|\xi_{2,k}\right\|_{\mu_{2,k}}\to\left\|\xi_{2}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
.
With
\xi_{2,k}\rightharpoonup\xi_{2}
and
\mu_{2,k}\to\bar{\mu}
, the convergence of second moments yields convergence in Wasserstein
\xi_{2,k}\to\xi_{2}
. Choosing
\hat{\xi}_{2,k}\in\hat{\normal}^{s}_{\Omega_{2}}(\mu_{2,k}^{\prime})
within
\varepsilon_{k}
of
\xi_{2,k}
(where
\mu_{2,k}^{\prime}\to\bar{\mu}
since
\mu_{2,k}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{B}_{W_{2}}(\mu_{k};2\varepsilon_{k})
and
\mu_{k}\to\bar{\mu}
), also
\hat{\xi}_{2,k}\to\xi_{2}
, and
Definition

2.24
gives
\xi_{2}\in\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
.

Step 6 (Ruling out
\lambda=0
).

Passing (
D.25
) to the limit via
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda
and
\left\|\xi_{1,k}\right\|_{\mu_{1,k}}\to\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}
gives
\max\{\lambda,\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}\}=1
. Suppose
\lambda=0
.
Then
\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{\bar{\mu}}=1
, so
\xi_{1}\neq\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}}
. With
\lambda=0
, (
D.26
) reads
\upsilon_{1}+\upsilon_{2}=0

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
. Since
\bar{x}=x_{1}=x_{2}

\gamma\text{-a.e.}
and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
are recovered as the
(x_{1},\upsilon_{1})
- and
(x_{2},\upsilon_{2})
-marginals of
\gamma
, we conclude that
\xi_{2}={(\pi_{1},-\pi_{2})}_{\#}{\xi_{1}}=-\xi_{1}
.
Hence,
\mathbf{0}_{\bar{\mu}}\neq\xi_{1}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\cap\left(-\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\right)
, contradicting assumption (i). Therefore,
\lambda>0
.

Step 7 (Conclusion).

As the normal cones are cones and
\lambda>0
, dividing (
D.26
) by
\lambda
gives

\bar{\xi}=\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\xi_{1}\,+_{\gamma}\,\tfrac{1}{\lambda}\xi_{2}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})+\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu}).

As
\bar{\xi}\in\normal_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
was arbitrary, we conclude
\normal_{\Omega_{1}\cap\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})\subseteq\normal_{\Omega_{1}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})+\normal_{\Omega_{2}}^{s}(\bar{\mu})
.
